# Rockett CAD work order

Revision of 2026-09-23, reviewed against origin/main at
`53e5600a545ee1ea6e94b354ad6a253eed35eed9`. All 432 original row IDs are retained.
This revision adds queue validation, browser acceptance and four release gates.
Mark adopted it through DEC-003. His decision rulings of 2026-09-23 are recorded
under Rulings.

## Purpose

Build self-hosted browser-based parametric CAD for designing and manufacturing
parts. B-Rep solids, editable sketches, stable references and feature-history
regeneration are the foundation. Assemblies, CNC/CAM with pluggable posts,
a KiCad bridge, electrical tools and additive export extend that foundation.
Third-party plugin loading follows a proven first-party API.

Users sign in. Their settings and workspace preferences live on the server
and follow them across browsers. Target one user initially and up to three
collaborators, with conflict-safe editing rather than real-time co-editing.
Run privately in Docker with separate development and production data.
Large-model support is demonstrated against declared fixtures and budgets.

## Rulings

Carried forward from Mark's rulings dated 2026-09-23:

- Support first-party and third-party plugins.
- Bridge to KiCad rather than build a native PCB editor.
- Support pluggable CNC post-processors; qualify individual controller dialects.
- Core licence: set by DEC-001 from the upstream author's grant; the upstream
  repository has no licence today. Respect each plugin author's terms and attribution.
- Store per-user profiles and settings on the server.
- Use local-volume storage first; share mounts, WebDAV and Nextcloud are later options.
- No artificial project limits. Keep resource budgets explicit and failures recoverable.
- Deploy privately. Keep real deployment settings and operational evidence outside this repo.
- This public repository contains application code and project documentation only.
- Reuse suitable code and support substantial UI customisation.
- Safe, verified commits may be pushed to origin/main, subject to the cost,
  publication and production approval boundaries in AGENTS.md.

Decisions ruled by Mark on 2026-09-23, each accepting its recommendation:

- DEC-002: in-app accounts with `node:crypto` scrypt hashes and an HttpOnly session cookie; no new dependency.
- DEC-003: Mark adopted this revision on 2026-09-23, replacing the version from `53e5600`.
- DEC-101: existing projects keep today's evaluation until an explicit, backed-up upgrade maps or reports every affected reference.
- DEC-201: option B, one exact-pinned JSON Schema builder and validator with no transitive dependencies. TypeBox is the candidate, subject to the adopting row's licence, size and tree review.
- DEC-202: stop at PERF-029 unless its `pick hover many-body` median exceeds 4 ms; then add exact-pinned `three-mesh-bvh` for face and body raycasts.
- DEC-203: jobs over 500 ms show the feature label, `done of total` and Cancel in the existing `viewport-hint` line; a cancelled or restarted kernel shows one error line; no new panel.
- DEC-301: the first non-local storage is an SMB or NFS share mounted at `/data`, served by `LocalStorage` with DOC-023's lock and mount probe.
- DEC-302: keep `/api` unversioned until PLUG sets the versioned plugin API.
- DEC-402: after AUTH-CP, Cloudflare Access becomes a second identity source by verifying its JWT with `node:crypto` and mapping the email to an existing user. Never trust the plain email header. No auto-created accounts.
- DEC-501: third-party modules are trusted code, installed only by folder drop, and stay disabled until an admin enables them, which records the folder hash; a hash change disables them. No signing, sandbox or marketplace yet.
- DEC-601: rigid, revolute and slider joints with optional limits, solved as a tree from grounded instances; no loops and no solver.
- DEC-602: components evaluate the latest revision in the same project, store the acknowledged revision, show an `updated` badge and recheck joint origins when they differ. Cross-project pinning is a later decision.
- DEC-611: the IR keeps tool change as a semantic event and each post maps it: GRBL 1.1 one file per tool, LinuxCNC `T` then `M6`.
- DEC-612: documents, libraries and the IR stay in mm; inch is a SET display unit and post output (`G20`) converted in the normaliser, proved by one inch golden.
- DEC-613: tool and machine libraries live per user on the server, copied by value into the document with a `libraryRef`.
- DEC-614: toolpaths generate on the server as kernel-worker jobs, cached by input fingerprint; the browser previews and simulates.
- DEC-616: third-party JavaScript posts wait until a real one exists, then run only in quickjs-emscripten with no fs or network and with memory and time limits.
- DEC-617: adaptive clearing and 3D surfacing wait until 2.5D has cut real parts.
- DEC-620: minimum KiCad 9, with fixtures for 9 and 10; older files get a 400 asking to save them in KiCad 9 or later.
- DEC-621: boards arrive by browser upload first; a read-only mounted share is one later row.
- DEC-622: KiCad owns the board outline by default; Rockett writes an outline DXF on request, and the owner is a per-link setting.
- DEC-623: `kicad-cli` only as a dev-only, digest-pinned oracle run by hand to make fixtures; never in the main image.
- DEC-640: electrical option (a): the schematic stays in KiCad, and Rockett shows board nets and connectors on the 3D model with cross-probing and pin tables.
- DEC-660: slicer handoff option (a): download a 3MF placed for the chosen printer. Network send waits until Mark names a printer host and accepts a slicer sidecar.
- DEC-004: UI pattern ruling. A row whose UI only composes existing patterns needs no separate design approval. Existing patterns: top-bar `icon-btn`; toolbar `tb-group`, `tb-btn` and `tb-select`; model tree `tree-section`, `tree-item`, `tree-empty` and eye toggle; timeline `tl-chip` with its `error`, `suppressed` and `rolledback` states and `tl-warn`; `DraggablePanel` dialogs with `dialog-body` and the KIT fields and footer (NumField, SelectField, CheckField, SelInfo, AxisField, LengthField, AngleField, DialogFooter); `context-menu` with `danger` items; `measure-row` read-outs; `project-row` lists and the centred `project-list-card` page; `dimmed` rows; `error-toast`, `error-banner`, `viewport-hint` and `sketch-status` messages; viewport hover and selection highlight. Rules: colours come only from theme tokens; no new layout region, dock, page layout, font or animation; every list states its empty, loading and error text; destructive actions confirm; the row cites DEC-004 and its handoff lists the controls it added. A new screen layout, panel region, workbench, gesture (drag, chord capture, viewport manipulation), viewport overlay kind or palette still needs its own approval. Mark may return any row built under this ruling; that reverses the row, not the ruling.
- DEC-401: covered by DEC-004; no separate auth design.
- DEC-205: dev-only interoperability readers, never shipped: `gltf-validator` exact-pinned as a root devDependency, and ezdxf, lib3mf and cadquery-ocp pinned by hash in a digest-pinned container run by hand like DEC-623. STEP export evidence uses OCP although it shares OCCT lineage; STEP import adds public NIST test models after their terms and sizes are checked.
- Password recovery is an offline `reset-password` command run with the app stopped; there is no web recovery route.
- AUTH-008 origins: `ROCKETT_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is a comma-separated list of full origins, each normalised with `new URL(x).origin`; startup fails with one line when it is unset or an entry holds a path or wildcard. The origin check never reads Host or forwarded headers, so behind a proxy the operator lists the public origin.
- DEC-403: Mark approved `docs/internals/design-settings.md` in full: the black default with `#66b3ff` as the one accent for UI and selection, amber hover `#ffd166` across the viewport, and teal construction planes `#4fd1c5`. No `classic` theme: today's grey palette fails the contrast targets and is recoverable from history.
- Greys: Mark on 2026-09-23, after seeing SET-011 on dev: bring back the graded greys. Surfaces and the viewport return to the grey values from before SET-011, and the accent, amber hover, teal planes and the contrast targets stay. A text token that misses its target is lightened; a surface is not darkened to fix it.
- DEC-406: Mark approved `docs/internals/design-projects.md` in full on 2026-09-23: one shared folder tree in `folders.json` with documents untouched, delete only on an empty folder, the breadcrumb and drag to move as new patterns, `/folders/<id>` links with Back to projects returning to the project's folder, a filter across the whole tree, and any signed-in user managing folders. Project create takes an optional `folderId`, so a new project lands in its folder in one call.
- Splitters: Mark on 2026-09-23 approved the Splitters section of `docs/internals/design-customisation.md` on its own, ahead of DEC-404; the rest of that design still waits on DEC-404. Panel sizes are user settings that follow the user across machines, never per-browser storage.
- README and cost: Mark on 2026-09-23: the README feature list is one short plain line per feature, and a row that changes what a user can do edits its line in the same change, so the README matches dev. Plugins (packages under `modules/`) and god files (source files over 500 lines) carry a measured cost: lines per function and a timing per public function, so tidy-up candidates show without reading the code.
- Session cookies are `__Host-` and `Secure` by default; `ROCKETT_COOKIE_SECURE=false` serves plain HTTP.
- A qualified controller dialect name is public; machine identity, profile, offsets and evidence stay private.
- Electrical reads KiCad board data through the `rockett.kicad.boardNets` module service, not a cross-module import.
- KiCad boards newer than the KiCad 10 fixture load with an `untested version` warning.
- Deferred REF-023: per DEC-101 version 1 projects stay valid; Mark upgrades a project when he chooses, using the REF-025 report.
- Deferred REF-024: waits on REF-023 and an owner compatibility policy.
- Deferred DOC-024: DEC-301 chose a mounted share; build WebDAV only if the host cannot mount one.

## Proposed

- Container stop: Node runs as PID 1 with no SIGTERM handler, so `docker stop` waits 10 s and kills with 137. Fix with a handler in `server/src/index.ts` that closes the server, or `init: true` in `docker-compose.yml` (found by OPS-002).
- Preview session: `closeProject` and `openProject` do not end the preview session (found by BUG-022).
- Asset misses: a missing `/assets/*` file gets the SPA fallback with 200 instead of 404 (found by OPS-004).
- Kernel leak: `arcEdge` does not delete its three `gp_Pnt` objects.
- Patch validation: BUG-014 checks the patch keys, not the merged feature (found by BUG-014).
- Error bodies: unknown `/api` paths and Express JSON body errors (malformed JSON, bodies over 50 MB) still answer without `code` (found by KIT-021).
- Distribution notices: the image carries no licence texts or `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, the LGPL opencascade.js source offer is not written, and the Node and Debian packages from the base image are not inventoried; all three block distribution, not intranet use (found by OPS-022).
- AUTH-002 timing: the hash was timed on the development desktop (70 ms median); the row asks for the dev host figure.
- First paint: `theme.css` no longer paints a background, so the page is white until `main.tsx` applies the tokens. Set the background in `client/index.html` (found by SET-008).
- Smoke listener: `server/src/index.ts` listens on all interfaces during the browser smoke test; bind it to loopback (found by OPS-030).
- Bench output: the PERF-001 row names `--outputJson`, which Vitest 5 dropped; the bench writes `server/dist/bench.json`, and the table test runs only under `npm run bench` (found by PERF-001).
- Worker lint: three `require-post-message-target-origin` oxlint warnings fire on `worker_threads`, where the rule does not apply (found by PERF-009).
- Node range: `engines.node` is `>=24` with no upper bound (found by OPS-002).

## Delivery and acceptance

REL-001 proves the core modelling product. REL-002 proves personal workspaces,
conflict handling and recovery. REL-003 proves the cross-workbench manufacturing
workflow, including the separately approved machine qualification. REL-004
checks release readiness. Implementation phase checkpoints do not replace these.

The phase index is: OPS hygiene; BUG defects; KIT shared primitives; DOC storage;
REF references; REL-001 core gate; AUTH identity; SET settings; UNDO history;
REG registries; CUST customisation; PERF scale; EXCH interchange; ASM assemblies;
PLUG modules; CAM machining; PCB bridge; ELEC electrical; PRINT additive;
REL release gates.

Rows below are in dependency order, rather than forcing all rows of one phase
to finish before a prerequisite from another phase. IDs retain their original
phase ownership. `scripts/check-work-order.sh --next` names the next row: the
first ready row in file order. Take that row; file order is the priority.
Rows parked by a decision, operator step, deferral or Proposed entry are skipped,
so independent work continues while they wait.

## Constraints

- Follow AGENTS.md, the coding standard and the complexity skill. Inspect the
  current implementation before choosing a design or applying a historical line reference.
- Original versions, counts, timings and source line numbers are baseline notes,
  not verified-current facts. Verify exact packages, licences, controller behaviour
  and file-format claims against pinned inputs and primary evidence when that row runs.
  Never encode a desired audit answer as an observed result.
- New tests use the existing workspace test folders; module tests use
  modules/<id>/test. OPS owns explicit discovery and DOM/browser separation.
- Every schema or naming change has old-format fixtures, a complete recoverable
  backup and a tested restore path. No automatic live-data migration is authorised here.
- Persist one owner per fact. Reuse KIT/REG/SET owners already introduced; later
  rows target their current locations, not deleted historical copies.
- Unknown module JSON survives semantically unchanged; source blobs remain byte-identical.
- Bound input bytes, nesting, decompression, jobs, retries, history and caches.
  A resource protection limit is not an artificial commercial project cap.
- Numeric CAD correctness, actual browser interaction and independent file
  interoperability are separate evidence. A DOM mock or self-generated golden
  cannot substitute for all three.
- Performance checks state fixture, anonymous hardware class, runtime, repetitions,
  warm-up and median/p95 budgets before judging a result. Keep machine details private.
- Visual changes use an approved design. Rows composing existing patterns follow DEC-004; a
  new screen layout, region, gesture, overlay kind or palette needs its own approval. Prepare designs before requesting approval.
- No paid action, public transmission, credential operation, production change or
  machine motion is authorised by a todo row. Apply the existing exact approval rules.
- Out of scope: native PCB routing, hosted cloud service, public deployment,
  mobile layouts and real-time multi-cursor editing. Do not add these indirectly.

## Agent loop

1. Read AGENTS.md, this header, the selected row and LAST-RUN.md. Confirm the
   active work order is adopted. Preserve unrelated work; inspect origin/HEAD
   and fast-forward only when safe without stashing or overwriting another task.
2. Run scripts/check-work-order.sh and take the row --next prints.
3. Claim only that row as in-flight. Check its decision outcomes, scope and
   dependencies. A decision choosing an alternative requires its dependent rows
   to be reconciled before execution; merely marking the decision done is insufficient.
4. Inspect named code. Write the rejecting proof and watch it fail for the
   intended reason, then implement and pass it. Documentation-only work uses
   a document/queue contract check; do not damage working code just to create red.
5. Run the focused proof, then npm run check and git diff --check.
   Run browser, benchmark, compatibility and restore checks where required.
   Read the full diff. A passing test command must discover the intended tests.
6. Mark done only with evidence of the complete Outcome. Write the short handoff
   and any README feature line the row changes in the same verified change, then commit only intended paths and publish only
   after the required public-source and indirect-cost checks. Never push unrelated commits.
7. Scope changes go under Proposed for Mark. A blocked row does not authorise
   weaker tests, arbitrary new scope or repeated speculative fixes. Continue only
   independent authorised work and report the dependency that blocks progress.

## Rows

Columns: ID, Outcome, Status, Depends, Scope, Check. No pipe inside a cell.
IDs are stable and never reused. Dependencies appear earlier in this file.
Status is todo, in-flight, done, decision, operator or deferred. A deferred row
needs a Rulings line `- Deferred <ID>: <reason>` and never satisfies a
dependency; its dependants wait.
A list item under Proposed that starts with row IDs and a colon, such as
`- A-001, A-002: reason`, parks those rows and their dependants until the entry
is resolved and removed. IDs after the colon are only references.

Done and in-flight require every dependency done. Operator means unfinished
human work, not a completion exemption. --complete rejects every unfinished
required row, including decisions and operator actions. A checkpoint depends on
every row of its phase except those listed in one `Explicitly excluded: A, B.`
sentence. Excluded rows remain outstanding and cannot be claimed as delivered.

Every row may also update its own status, the handoff, directly affected tests,
and the existing document owning a changed contract. Source paths in Scope
are current ownership boundaries, not permission to ignore necessary proof.
If a refactor has moved an owner, update the row path before work. Split an
oversized row without losing its original outcome or acceptance gate.

## AUTH, identity

| ID | Outcome | Status | Depends | Scope | Check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AUTH-001 | `shared/src/auth.ts` exports `User` with `id`, `username`, `displayName`, `role` (`admin` or `member`), `status` (`active` or `disabled`), `createdAt` and `modifiedAt`. `server/src/auth/userStore.ts` keeps `UserRecord` (a `User` plus `passwordHash`) in one `JsonStore<T>` record, `users/users.json`, with its own schema version and migration table, and exposes `list`, `get`, `findByUsername`, `create` and `update`. Usernames are NFKC-normalised, lowercased and must match `^[a-z0-9._-]{1,32}$`; a duplicate is a 409. `toPublicUser` is the only way a record leaves the store and it drops `passwordHash`. | ID | Outcome | Status | Depends | Scope | Check |

| ASM-001 | Document assemblies beside part documents using KIT rigid placements. Components follow DEC-602: the latest revision plus an acknowledged revision for the update badge. Define per-document cache identity, joint frames, nested-instance paths, BOM and interference derivation, and an approved assembly workbench design. Exclude in-context editing, motion studies, contact and cross-project components. | todo | DOC-CP, KIT-CP | `docs/internals/assemblies.md` | the note exists and names every type and rule in this Outcome; `scripts/check-work-order.sh` passes |
| DEC-601 | Mark picks the joint scope. Options: (a) grounded plus rigid only; (b) rigid, revolute and slider, each with a value and optional min and max limits, solved as a tree from grounded instances with no loops and no solver; (c) the Fusion set (rigid, revolute, slider, cylindrical, pin-slot, planar, ball) with a constraint solver and motion. Recommended: (b). It covers enclosures, lids, hinges and fixture slides with a pure tree walk, and (c) needs a loop-closing solver that no current workflow asks for. | done | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |
| DEC-602 | Mark picks how components track part changes. Options: (a) pin a revision and evaluate that historical revision until the user updates; (b) always evaluate the latest revision in the same project, store the last acknowledged revision, show an `updated` badge and recheck joint origins when they differ. Recommended: (b). Pinning needs every referenced old revision kept evaluable, one kernel engine per revision, and one user in one project gains little from it. Cross-project pinning can come later as its own decision. | done | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |

API versions: additive within a major, one major of deprecation. Disable and hide: what app disable and user hide mean. Missing modules: the document opens editable, the module's features show as errors, and its data is kept as equal JSON values. ## CAM, CNC and CAM

| ID | Outcome | Status | Depends | Scope | Check |

| DEC-610 | Mark identifies the first machine/controller and qualifies that exact post and machine profile. Pluggable posts means extensibility, not universal controller compatibility. Other dialects remain candidate exports until independently verified. Keep actual machine identity, offsets and operational evidence outside the public repository. | decision | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |
| DEC-611 | Mark picks tool-change handling. Options: (a) manual pause (`M0` plus a comment) inside one file; (b) one output file per tool; (c) `M6` for an automatic changer. Recommended: the IR keeps tool change as a semantic event and each post's capability picks the mapping: GRBL 1.1 defaults to one file per tool because it rejects `M6`; LinuxCNC uses `T` then `M6`. | done | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |
| DEC-612 | Mark decides inch support. Recommended: documents, libraries and the IR stay in mm; inch appears only as a SET display unit and as post output (`G20`) converted in the normaliser, proved by one inch golden. Inch tool libraries are entered in inches and stored in mm. | done | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |
| DEC-613 | Mark decides where tool and machine libraries live. Options: per user on the server, per project, or only inside each document. Recommended: per user in the server library store (app-wide until AUTH lands), copied by value into the document with a `libraryRef` when used, so a project stays self-contained when moved. | done | - | `WORK-ORDER.md` | the ruling is recorded under Rulings |
| DEC-614 | Mark decides where toolpaths are generated. Recommended: on the server as kernel-worker jobs, because region extraction needs OCCT; results are cached by input fingerprint on the server; the browser only previews and simulates.
